# Visualizer sync regression

Base: `7d89d9b20c`

Task V3: complete (this commit, base 05b4adad89, moved the Spectrum analyzer ahead of ReplayGain so stored Track/Album gain cannot attenuate visual energy while audible normalization remains unchanged; the exact WAKE UP Track-ReplayGain profile improved from 43% to 93% visible peak at 16 seconds and from 37% to 87% at 36 seconds; workspace 1312 core + 901 GNOME non-display + 81 Linux platform + 45 stems tests passed, 280 display tests were intentionally ignored, and one unrelated device-sync test failed once before passing both isolated and in the full rerun)
Task V4: complete (this commit, base 9aa28f69fb, added a faster-than-realtime song-corpus profiler and calibrated Bars against WAKE UP, To the Hellfire, and Sun//Eater so compressed metal retains headroom while the reported 16- and 36-second WAKE UP windows peak near 80-82% mean and 88% tallest-bar height with 16-32 ms best alignment; the quieter half now produces 0.5% false-full frames in WAKE UP and at most 6.5% across the Lorna Shore corpus, a three-tick pressure hold preserves fluid short-hit attack, and selected passive view tabs again leave Space to global Play/Pause; workspace 1314 core + 901 GNOME non-display + 81 Linux platform + 45 stems tests passed, 281 display tests were intentionally ignored, the targeted Xvfb regression and isolated real-key CUA song-visuals scenario passed, core purity and architecture/UX gates passed, and cargo audit reported only the accepted RUSTSEC-2024-0436 warning)

# Thin core and headless MCP — Stage 1

Task 1.1: complete (commit 650cdc5536, base 9ed9c731a8, made the runtime a binding design instead of a parked idea: section 9 of multi-frontend-core.md draws the ownership line so Beschluss 1 keeps the database and the runtime gets only the state that never reaches it, and specifies the four lifecycle states with complete transitions, an exclusive lease taken before any effect is opened, D-Bus activation as the single start path, snapshot-then-deltas reconnection with no replay and no buffered commands, an idle shutdown that refuses to interrupt work, four shared error categories and a per-resource capability matrix recording today's state; section 2.1 gained a forward note and the user-visible half became RUN-1..RUN-5 [geplant] in the new UX section AG; UX traceability with 239 active rules passed; the window-close question is deliberately open and marked as a Regelvorschlag because it is a product decision, not an architectural consequence)
Task 1.2: complete (commit 8ef1c2fee9, base 650cdc5536, replaced the sixteen-field positional device-sync tuple — declared once in reprise-platform-linux and again in reprise-mcp, with seven consecutive u64s in one nested row — by named types in a new reprise-runtime-protocol crate that both sides share; snapshots now cross D-Bus as a{sv} dictionaries keyed by field name so a reorder is unobservable and an unset Option is an absent key rather than a (bool, T) pair, PROTOCOL_VERSION plus a ProtocolVersion property give the seam a handshake that refuses a foreign major version with an actionable message, and command enums stay in-process because D-Bus has no sum type; nine schema tests pin round trips including nested dictionaries and sparse optionals, the checked-in wire field names and path freedom, and the existing private-bus integration round trip now runs end to end through the named types; the protocol crate is optional in reprise-mcp behind its mpris feature so check-architecture.sh's rule that the default MCP build depends on reprise-core alone still holds; format, strict workspace clippy with and without all features, rustdoc, the full workspace suite, the all-features MCP suite, architecture, accessibility, input, motion, UX traceability, QA linters, core purity, Rust file-size enforcement and audit with only accepted RUSTSEC-2024-0436 passed)
Task 1.2 review fix: complete (this commit, base 8ef1c2fee9, from an adversarial review of the protocol change: the field-by-field migration of the sixteen-field tuple and its four nested rows was confirmed correct with no dropped, swapped or polarity-inverted field, but two contradictions were real. `is_compatible_with` refused a peer with a lower minor version, which contradicts the crate's own documented rule that a minor bump only adds optional fields an older peer may omit — it would have hard-failed the most ordinary upgrade sequence, a client updated on disk while the older runtime still runs; only the major version decides now, and the minor stays for a client that wants to skip a feature knowingly. Section 9.8 of the architecture plan asserted that playback and device tools are in the default MCP build, which the shipped `mpris` feature gate contradicts; the rule is now stated as the target it is, with Task 3.4 named as the place it becomes true)
Task 1.3: complete (this commit, base a7f47576ee, gave the thin-frontend goal a number instead of an intention: scripts/check-frontend-thinness.sh counts direct rusqlite, filesystem and thread use plus business-worker files in reprise-gnome and pins each at its measured value as both ceiling and floor, so growth fails and an extraction fails too until it lowers the budget in the same commit; GStreamer and zbus are bans rather than budgets because the frontend already reaches zero on both. Counting strips comments and #[cfg(test)] blocks — without the first, ui/mod.rs's own documentation of the zbus ban would trip the check enforcing it, and without the second, writing a test would be punished; the block is skipped rather than the file truncated, so production code after a test module stays measured, which the first draft missed and which is worth 27 rusqlite and one thread use. The thirteen files carrying #[allow(dead_code)] are pinned per file so a new escape hatch has to justify itself in the commit that adds it. The ratchet was verified against deliberate violations, not just assumed to work. Two plan items are honestly not delivered: cargo-machete is not installed, so the check reports SKIPPED instead of passing quietly, and -D unreachable-pub is deferred with its measurement recorded — it is 1240 sites in reprise-gnome, roughly 60% of them pub const string-table entries in modules already private to crate::ui, so it is a mechanical mass edit for its own quiet moment rather than a lint flip. Wired into check-merge-readiness.sh and the qa-linter policy list; format, strict workspace clippy with and without all features, rustdoc, the full workspace suite, the all-features MCP suite, architecture, UX traceability and QA linters passed)
Stage 1: complete (tasks 1.1-1.3 plus one review fix; merged into dev together with Stage 0 as PRs #110 and #111, dev now at 7014d364e6). Acceptance was local and not GitHub: the account's Actions cannot start jobs, and the repository's branch protection turns out to be unavailable on its plan anyway, so scripts/check-merge-readiness.sh against the current origin/dev is the gate — it passed after merging the newer dev (PR #109) into the stack, with the new thinness ratchets unchanged. Branches deleted, worktree moved to feature/thin-core-state-machines, and the nightly build was triggered and verified against the installed binary rather than its log.

# Visualizer bass glow

Bass glow rework: complete (this commit, base 323859faf3, moved the glow layer off the CAVA bands, which auto-sensitivity re-normalizes, so a quiet sung passage climbed to the same band values as a drop and the two broad glows plus their inner auras were lit almost permanently; PR #82 had made that visible rather than caused it by compressing the trigger window from [0.42, 1.0] to [0.36, 0.49], i.e. from 58% of the scale to 13%, which turned a ramp into a switch. The new reprise-core BassPressureDetector measures the same mono PCM with no gain of its own — 30-150 Hz Butterworth band, block RMS in true dBFS, and a 2 s baseline of the track's own bass — and derives one value a rhythmic kick reaches and a second that only a sustained breakdown lifts; SpectrumFrame carries both, the player feeds the detector alongside CAVA and resets it on stream change and DISCONT, and bars.rs scales the two glow layers straight off them, so the engine holds no bass heuristic at all. Calibrated against decoded audio of The Browning "WAKE UP" and Lorna Shore "To the Hellfire" rather than guessed: their quiet intros measure around -40 dBFS and their walls of sound around -14 dBFS, so absolute level alone separates them by 25 dB, and the checked-in bass_pressure_corpus example replays raw PCM through the real detector to re-check the numbers. Rendering the full chain at three moments of "WAKE UP" confirms the intended grading on screen — intro 0.00, rhythmic passage 0.35 with no aura, breakdown 0.89 with aura 0.95. AC-22 is replaced by AC-23 and its 37 tests re-hung, and the visual now names the analysis it reacts to in a 2x2 readout under the canvas, laid out that way because four columns broke the fixed 300 px panel of NPP-1 — caught by three red display tests, not by inspection. Format, strict workspace clippy, the full workspace suite, 178 rule-named plus 26 motion plus 10 CSS display tests, architecture, accessibility, input, motion, UX traceability with 239 active rules and frontend thinness passed; check-merge-readiness.sh was not run because the worktree carries unrelated untracked plan files)

Bass glow rework, corrections and follow-up: the entry above was written before the work finished and two of its numbers are wrong — 39 tests were re-hung from AC-22 onto AC-23, not 37, and the display run it cites as 178 rule-named tests became 180 once the two new readout guards existed. Three things also happened after it: driving the running app through cua-driver (isolated Xvfb/openbox/D-Bus session, its own cua-driver daemon, a copy of the library) showed the readout's second row silently clipped — caption above value needs 88 px where the strip under the canvas offers about 52 — and its captions truncated to "BA…" once they did fit, so caption and value now share a line and the levels read in whole decibels; both bounds are now pinned by measuring the widget against the real panel width and free strip height with the production stylesheet loaded. The Visual tab traded its speaker icon for rising bars, since Adwaita ships no equalizer glyph and a speaker says where audio comes out rather than what it looks like. A rust-reviewer pass approved the change with no critical or high findings; its two actionable notes were applied — the biquad state is snapped to zero at 1e-30 so a fade-out cannot decay into subnormal floats on the streaming thread, and SpectrumFrame::with_bass_pressure is #[must_use]. Merged as PR #116 (dev 49834b78f2) after the full display set ran green post-merge with origin/dev. One investigation closed negative and is worth recording so nobody repeats it: the AT-SPI tree appeared to show three SongVisualizer instances, but counting canvases in the real widget tree returns exactly one — cua-driver traverses the ScrolledWindow/Viewport subtree more than once and assigns separate element indices, so the duplication is in the snapshot, not the app.

Promotion to main: complete (PR #118, origin/main d1de9e3bfb, promoting dev 49834b78f2 — 108 commits, 261 files, +27400/-13713 since the previous stable revision: the visualizer bass-pressure rework, the thin-core runtime contracts and state machines, Android sync transfer profiles, device-sync step labels, releases with Bandcamp discography gaps, the QA viewport and responsive-player regressions, idle visuals with restored lyrics, and the English UX rulebook. scripts/check-merge-readiness.sh ran complete against origin/main and ended green: architecture, multi-frontend core boundaries, frontend lint, core purity, Rust file-size enforcement, the full workspace suite plus doc-tests, 180 rule-named plus 26 motion plus 10 CSS display tests each in its own process, accessibility, input parity, motion tokens, the GStreamer device-sync runtime, UX traceability with 239 active rules, and the dependency audit with only the accepted RUSTSEC-2024-0436. One honest note: stats_10_section_gaps_stay_equal_when_top_tracks_expand_and_collapse is load-sensitive rather than broken — it waits a fixed 400 ms for a GtkRevealer transition before asserting is_child_revealed(), which fails under a four-way parallel display run and passes serially as the gate runs it; converting it to wait on the transition instead of a deadline is open work and predates this promotion)

# Device sync log

Sync log: complete (PR #123, base dev 75f75a09d8, from a live question the app could not answer — a playlist that synced as 200 tracks showed 104 on the phone, and the only record was one last_synced_at per playlist plus two warnings in the whole sync path. Runs are now recorded in two new tables behind schema v40: sync_runs holds device, transfer profile, planned count and the balance of copied/skipped/removed/failed with the reason a run did not complete, and sync_events itemizes only files that deviated, so a 278-file run leaves a handful of rows rather than hundreds. Three decisions carry the design: the entry opens at the start rather than closing at the end, so a run whose session dies is marked interrupted by the next one instead of vanishing — "it never finished" being the answer someone actually wants; removals are itemized even though the balance counts them, because the mirror owns Music/Reprise under MTP-17 and what it deleted is the question that gets asked; and every log write is best-effort, so recording can never fail a transfer. Retention is the last thirty runs, ageing out with their deviations. The device page grows a "Recent transfers" card of expandable rows, with the wording in the frontend because the core deliberately holds none. Two guards earned their keep on the way: the schema-version pin went red on the v40 migration exactly as intended, and the frontend-thinness ratchet caught the log helpers naming Connection in their signatures — rusqlite use in reprise-gnome had grown 537 to 540 — so they now take the runtime instead and the count is back at its budget, which is also the truer shape since they never needed a database handle. Rule MTP-20 activated. Ten core tests, seven wording tests, one display test against the real page, the full workspace suite, format, strict clippy, architecture, accessibility, frontend thinness and UX traceability with 242 active rules passed. Honestly open: the log proves itself only against a real device on the next sync, and it cannot explain the run that prompted it)
