Incident T6-verification: an earlier claim in this stage that the rule-named display gate was green was wrong — the gate reports "failed: N of M" rather than "FAILED", so grepping for the latter reads as green while sixteen tests were failing on a mid-refactor tree. Read the block after "== display test summary ==" instead. Two apparent regressions (stats_17, tag_6) turned out to be environment flakes under parallel build load, failing with "Failed to initialize GTK" and passing in isolation on both HEAD and base.
Task T6-HA (Turn 6c/Block H, MCP discovery): complete (commit 9308c9e8 merged as a no-ff merge, base 50231d3d, added the capability-gated music_search_sources tool exposing provider-separated discovery over MCP under the existing sources:manage gate, honouring SRC-6 by querying exactly one provider and SRC-9 by omitting an absent subscriber count entirely; the already-subscribed filtering was moved out of the GNOME crate into reprise-core as podcasts::discovery and radio::search::filter_new_stations so GUI and MCP share one projection instead of drifting, with the existing rule-named tests moved alongside it; no new UX rule was needed because SRC-6 and SRC-9 are already written surface-neutral; format, strict clippy, the full workspace suite including six new MCP integration tests, UX traceability with 250 active rules, architecture and core purity passed after the merge)
Decision T6-HA-url: the MCP discovery response returns each candidate URL with userinfo and fragment stripped and non-http(s) schemes rejected, but deliberately keeps the query string, because SRC-5's own test proves a feed's query string can be part of its identity and stripping it would point a follow-up add at the wrong feed; artwork URLs are omitted entirely. This is a documented deviation from the blanket "no query strings" wording in docs/plans/podcasts-youtube-radio-turn6.md; the owner confirmed it on 2026-07-28, so the plan's wording is the part that is wrong and was corrected.
Task T6-G1 (Turn 7b, Online sources page and global gate): complete (commit d5467ee6 plus follow-up 4803761e, base 12afbde4, added the Online sources preferences page with a global master and independent YouTube, Podcasts and Radio blocks, promoted YouTube to its own peer ModuleDescriptor with id "youtube" and retired podcasts.youtube_enabled outright since there are no installations to migrate, and introduced reprise_core::online_sources as the single authority whose network_allowed ANDs the global switch with each module; NET-1 was replaced by NET-1a and SET-8 was added; the design's Phone sync block was deliberately left out because its rows configure Block E sync work that does not exist yet)
Incident T6-G1-gap: the first G1 commit gated only the YouTube search, so with the switch off podcast search still reached Apple and radio search still reached radio-browser while the page promised "no requests, no downloads, nothing hidden". Both dialogs now decide at one submit choke point before any provider work, state the refusal rather than failing silently, and treat a failed lookup as not allowed; the podcast dialog's SRC-6 and NET-1a guards were merged into one pure, display-free testable function. The lesson is that a privacy claim in copy needs a test per call path, not per feature.
Harness repair T6: the rule-named display gate now retries a test once, on a different Xvfb server number, when and only when it failed with GTK's "Failed to initialize GTK" — an environment fault that produced three false regressions in one day and that always passed on an isolated re-run. Any other failure is still reported unchanged, and a retry logs a visible line. The retry did not trigger in its verifying run, so it is guarded but not yet proven in practice.
Fix T6-play7b: play_7b_podcast_episode_table_scrolls_inside_the_player_bar_boundary had been the gate's one standing failure since before this stage. It compared ScrolledWindow::child() with the table directly, but GtkScrolledWindow hosts a non-scrollable child inside a GtkViewport of its own, so the assertion compared a viewport with a box and could never hold. It now asserts that the table is what the scroller hosts.
Task T6-F1/F3 (6e, offline contract): complete (commit cfc3e331, base eade62d8, wrote NET-3 as the app-wide offline contract covering cached, empty, queued, interrupted, authentication, rate limit and provider failure, kept [geplant] because only part of it is implemented; NET-3a adds a pure display-free projection in reprise_core::connectivity that answers from connectivity plus local availability what a row reads and what an action does, with phone sync falling out of it because MTP transfer is local; NET-3b implements the Radio exception, where a live stream cannot be deferred and Play offers "No connection · Retry" instead of queueing; connectivity is an explicit injectable value rather than inferred from a failed request; LYR-3 was deliberately left out of the migration because it describes a disabled module rather than an offline state; format, strict clippy, full workspace suite, core purity, UX traceability with 253 active rules, architecture, accessibility, input parity and motion passed)
Note T6-F-scope: F2's queue execution and automatic run-on-reconnect, F4's dialog offline mode, and 7f's sync skip notice are modelled but not wired, and remain open. Closes the specification half of issue #107.
Task T6-E1 (7a/7e/7f, sync target and wanted state model): complete (commit 95c65bc4, base dc2597c2, replaced the single managed device folder with three named per-device targets carrying StorageID, path, activation and an optional cap in new table device_sync_targets, added oldest-first cap eviction as a pure function, and introduced wanted_on_device so that marking an episode for the phone before it has a file defers the download instead of demanding the user download first; the global-rules versus per-device-folders split from 7b/7e is expressed structurally — SyncTarget deliberately carries no content-selection field, and its doc comment says why — and the Android media-scanner reasoning behind /Podcasts/Reprise and /Music/Reprise-YouTube is recorded so the defaults are not tidied away later; wanted_on_device composes with connectivity::deferrable_action_outcome from NET-3a rather than duplicating it; new rules MTP-18, MTP-19 and MTP-20 after checking MTP-1 through MTP-17 for collisions; schema moved 41 to 43 with no migration path needed since there are no installations; format, strict clippy, 1536 core tests and the full workspace suite, core purity, cached audit with only accepted RUSTSEC-2024-0436, UX traceability with 256 active rules, architecture, accessibility, input parity, motion, and the display gate at 184 of 184 all passed)
Note T6-E1-followup: POD-8 still hardcodes "downloaded RSS episodes only" and the old single-folder paths. It is not yet contradictory because query_candidates_for_device is untouched and wanted_on_device is inert, but POD-8 and docs/plans/android-sync.md must be updated in E2/E4 when the new model actually replaces the old resolution.
Task T6-E2/E3 (7a/7c, selection and category diff): complete (commit 3ff96687, base 8c1671ce, added MTP-21 for a pure per-category intended-file-set projection and MTP-22 for a per-category plan reading with an aggregated balance; copy and remove stay separate in both files and bytes and has_work counts files rather than bytes, so a deletions-only sync reports work while moving zero bytes — the defect design 7c names, where "3 changes · 0 B" reads as nothing to do while three files are deleted; an item wanted without a file yet is counted apart and never folded into files_to_copy so the balance cannot promise a transfer that will not happen; a category distinguishes switched-off from evaluated-to-zero from could-not-be-evaluated rather than flattening all three into one zero; the existing playlist mirror plan is translated into the same shape instead of being diffed a second time; format, strict clippy, full workspace suite, core purity, UX traceability with 258 active rules, architecture, accessibility, input parity and motion passed)
Incident T6-fil9: fil_9_filter_changes_center_the_visible_playing_track fails with a stable off-by-one-row result (actual 680, expected 614.5, a gap of about one row height). It is NOT a regression from this stage: it fails identically at dc2597c2, the last commit where the display gate reported 184/184, and no commit on this branch touched crates/reprise-gnome/src/ui/track_list/ or scroll_center.rs. Manual runs replicating the gate's full environment still fail, so the difference is not the private XDG roots. Filed as issue #122 rather than chased inside Block E. Note the contrast with the GTK-init flakes: those print "Failed to initialize GTK" and are retried by the gate; fil_9 produces a real assertion and must not be treated as environmental.
Task T6-E4 (7a, transfer): complete (commit 63ba5048, base 4d45a21a, routed the transfer through the three named per-device targets instead of one managed root, switched YouTube phone sync on by making every RSS-only assumption kind-aware rather than special-casing YouTube at one call site, kept music on the transfer profile while copying podcast and YouTube audio 1:1 because they are already Opus or AAC (MTP-24), made the size cap real with oldest-first eviction before an overflowing transfer starts (MTP-25), and added MTP-23 for the write and delete contract; POD-8 asserted both RSS-only selection and a single fixed folder, so it was replaced by POD-12 with its test re-pointed rather than left contradicting the new rules; docs/plans/android-sync.md was updated; all tests drive the recording DeviceBackend double so the gate never needs a phone; format, strict clippy, full workspace suite, core purity, UX traceability with 261 active rules, architecture, accessibility, input parity, motion and the device-sync GStreamer check passed)
Incident T6-commit-sweep: while an agent was working uncommitted in the same worktree, a `git add -A` from the coordinating session swept its entire in-progress E4 change into a commit labelled as a docs-only change. Caught by inspecting the commit's stat, then split apart with a soft reset so the docs commit carries only the doc file and the agent's work was re-verified and committed on its own. The lesson: never `git add -A` in a worktree where a subagent is working; either give the agent its own worktree or stage explicit paths.
Task T6-E5/E7 (7a/7c, device view and sidebar card): complete (commit 9562eda2, base ea22d9ce, gave the device page a content panel with a category-segmented storage bar carrying a hatched "Incoming this sync" segment, a "Device contents never verified" state with a Scan device action reusing the existing inspect call rather than a second scan mechanism, a Content section per named target and a Next synchronization panel reading MTP-22 per category; the sidebar card's blended "N changes · X to transfer" line is gone, replaced by exactly one of four leading sentences projected from the balance, with the full figures moved to the tooltip; the deletions-only case now reads "3 to remove · frees 148.0 MiB" and its test additionally asserts the string never contains "0 B", so the defect design 7c names cannot silently return; the 2026-07-28 addendum is honoured structurally — selection summary and cap are read-only displays of the global Preferences rules and the section's only switch is SyncTarget::enabled, which MTP-28 states as binding text; MTP-11 described an idle card that no longer exists and is marked [ersetzt durch MTP-29]; new rules MTP-26 through MTP-29 after checking MTP-1 through MTP-25 for collisions; verified independently of the agent's report: format, strict clippy, 1566 core and 1095 GNOME tests with none failing, core purity, and all five script gates passed)
Task T6-E-autostart (7a, MTP-30): complete (commit f2567a87, base 9562eda2, the "Sync automatically when this phone connects" switch shipped in 9562eda2 was a dead control — persisted as schema v44 with DEFAULT 1 and rendered into the UI, but no code path ever read it to decide anything, because design 7a and the plan both specify the control under E5 while no block owned the behaviour and F2 covers network reconnect rather than device connect; the owner chose to implement it with the default left on; the decision is the pure should_auto_start over AutoStartFacts in reprise-core, requiring the first refresh after connect, the switch on, a verified scan, error-free planning, an idle connected device and real work per the existing SyncBalance, with the balance reused through a shared category_readings() rather than derived a second time; the GTK side gathers facts in one short borrow, drops it, and only then calls sync_now, mirroring the existing resume_planned hook because sync_now re-enters GTK; a refused or failed automatic start stays silent apart from a tracing warning since the user pressed nothing; verified that the trigger cannot repeat — apply_devices only enqueues a refresh when a device actually connects or reconnects, so a repeated poll of an already-connected device enqueues nothing; format, strict clippy, 2997 workspace tests with none failing, core purity, five script gates, cached audit with only accepted RUSTSEC-2024-0436, and the rule-named display gate at "failed: 0 of 184" all passed)
Note T6-E-autostart-default: turning the switch on by default is a real behavioural change, not a cosmetic default — it broke 33 existing tests that drive sync_now by hand and had begun racing an automatic start. Those fixtures now set sync_automatically: false explicitly with a rule reference. In production the same default means a connected device with pending work begins transferring unprompted; this is the owner's explicit decision, recorded here because it is the kind of default that looks like an accident when read later.
Correction T6-fil9: the incident entry above concluded that fil_9 is not environmental because its failure numbers are constant. That conclusion was wrong, and the reasoning was the trap — a row height is a discrete layout state, so an unsettled layout is off by exactly one row every time, which makes constant numbers evidence of nothing. A controlled re-test (idle twice, then twelve CPU busy-loops applied and released under a shell trap) passed both idle runs and failed one of two loaded runs with the identical 680 versus 614.5. fil_9 is a flaky test sensitive to CPU contention. The "not a regression, fails at dc2597c2 too" half of the original finding stands unchanged. Issue #122 was corrected and retitled rather than left carrying the wrong diagnosis.
Incident T6-cpu-leak: this machine was carrying 48 orphaned CPU busy-loops leaked by an unrelated fil_9 load experiment in another worktree — four batches of twelve, running 3 h 43 min at a load average of about 52 on eight cores, which is very likely why the display gate's GTK-init flakes appeared in the first place and certainly why every fil_9 failure in this stage clustered in that window. The experiment applies hogs and kills them at the end, but its parent shell was reaped first, so the kill never ran and the spinners were reparented to systemd. Removed by verifying each PID individually (command line, orphaned parent, no children) rather than by a broad pattern kill, after an earlier broad pkill in this stage had hit builds belonging to the user. Any future load experiment must arm a shell trap so the hogs die with the script.
Task T6-E6 (7d, device folder browser): complete (commit 0a26675d, base 3b089e56, gave the three named per-device sync targets an editing surface — "Change folder…" in the Content section (MTP-28) opens a browser with storage selection, folder tree navigation, "New folder", a target preview, a warning when the chosen folder sits inside the authoritative Playlists tree (MTP-17), and "Reset to default"; every decision (storage classification, target preview, playlist-conflict warning, reset, and the MTP-32 relocate-vs-recopy choice) is a pure function in reprise_core::device_sync::browser, reusing MTP-18's target_storage_transition rather than re-deriving it; GTK only gathers facts through four new DeviceBackend methods (list_storages/list_folders/create_folder/move_folder, each with an "unavailable" default) and obeys; reprise-platform-linux resolves storage and folder contents fresh from gio::File enumeration every call (no MTP handle is ever persisted) and derives a stable StorageId from each storage's GVfs-reported name since GVfs exposes no raw PTP StorageID; a device that refuses folder creation at a storage's own root surfaces DeviceIoError::CannotCreateAtStorageRoot distinctly; new rules MTP-31 and MTP-32 after checking MTP-1 through MTP-30 for collisions, and MTP-28's closing sentence (which deferred this browser to E6) was updated in place; the FakeBackend test double was extended for browsing/folder creation and split into its own device_sync_fake_backend.rs to keep device_sync_runtime_tests.rs under the file-size gate, so no test needs a real phone; format, strict clippy, the full workspace suite (1596 core, 1108 GNOME non-display plus 327 display-only ignores, 104 Linux platform, 49 stems, full MCP suite), core purity, UX traceability at 268 active rules, architecture, accessibility semantics and input parity all passed). The rule-named Xvfb display gate was intentionally not run by this task, per the coordinating session's instruction that it runs it separately.
Task T6-C1 (Block C, remote image module): complete (commit cdd73a53 plus fixes 1ec22016, 077b32e0 and 5-… see below, base 3b089e56, added reprise_core::remote_image with a pure resolve over an injected fetcher, a bounded on-disk cache and the source_images module, and unblocked the image parts of A2 and B1 which had been shipping on the glyph fallback; the commit also closed a live privacy hole rather than only adding a feature — before it, podcasts_groups, youtube_channel_detail and the radio search passed real remote URLs straight into SourceImage, whose set_url called source_artwork::fetch with no gate check at all, so switched-off online sources still fetched artwork; new rule SRC-11 with a test per call path)
Fix T6-C1-cachehit: SRC-11 states that a cache hit is always shown regardless of the gate and remote_image::resolve implements exactly that, reading the on-disk cache before it consults the flag — but the widget returned before ever reaching it, so a closed gate hid images already on disk that needed no request. The divergence was safe yet the rule is binding, and the cost was invisible: the memory cache is empty at startup, so every restart with sources off dropped artwork it had already downloaded. The five existing src_11 widget tests could not catch it because with an empty cache the fallback looks identical either way; the new test populates the cache through the public core path first and fails without the fix (commit 1ec22016).
Task T6-E6 (7d, device target-folder browser): complete (commits 0a26675d and 33e43649, base 1ec22016, storage selection, a folder tree, New folder, target preview, the playlist-nesting warning and Reset to default, with every decision a pure projection in device_sync::browser and the GTK side only rendering; GVfs exposes no raw PTP StorageID, so derive_storage_id hashes the reported storage name — stable across reconnects for one phone but not bit-identical to the wire value, the one deliberate compromise and documented as such; new rules MTP-31 and MTP-32, MTP-28's "path is read-only text" sentence resolved)
Review T6-codex: an external reviewer (Codex CLI, read-only sandbox, no writes and no builds) audited ea22d9ce..HEAD — 52 files, ~5500 added lines, scoped deliberately to this window rather than the whole 43-commit branch. It returned six P1 defects and three contract gaps; every one was verified against the code before being acted on, and all six P1s are now fixed with a red-then-green proof per fix. What the gates could not see is the point: all of it passed fmt, clippy, 3033 workspace tests, five script gates and a 191-of-191 display gate.
Fix T6-review-storage: E6's browser was largely cosmetic. PlannedWork carried only paths and never the storage_id, every DeviceBackend write went through storage_root() with its hard "prefer internal" rule, and inspection walked hardcoded Music/Reprise, Music/Reprise-YouTube and Podcasts/Reprise. Choosing an SD card and /Music/YT therefore transferred to internal storage anyway, and the next scan did not recognise the chosen folder as that category's inventory, so it reported the category empty and planned the same files again. Storage now threads through the whole transfer chain and inspection resolves each target's own storage and path (commit 667b567d, rules MTP-33 through MTP-35 for this and the two below).
Fix T6-review-deadswitch: remove_deleted was the THIRD persisted-but-never-read control found on this branch, after sync_automatically (MTP-30) and the ungated image fetch. target_podcast_plan passed a hardcoded true, so turning the switch off and unsubscribing still queued the device file for deletion. The pattern is the lesson: a switch that renders and persists reads as finished, and nothing in the gate battery disagrees. Any new setting needs a test that asserts the behaviour differs between its two positions, not that the row exists.
Fix T6-review-race: the folder browser started an uncancelled listing per navigation and appended results unconditionally, so a slow /Music listing could land under /Podcasts and a row click then navigated to a path that does not exist. Now behind a generation token, the same shape cover_loader.rs and source_image.rs already use for recycled rows.
Fix T6-review-savefail: a refused Save logged a warning and closed the folder browser anyway, so a save rejected because the device was busy or gone looked successful and the choice was silently lost. It now reports inline through the same error surface MTP-31 already uses for a refused New folder and keeps the dialog open.
Fix T6-review-gatestale: the artwork workers captured the gate value at enqueue time, so tasks queued while online sources were on kept fetching after the switch went off (commit 077b32e0 moved the decision to a process-wide atomic read immediately before the fetch). That fix was itself incomplete: the atomic was published only from queue_artwork, so it flipped only when somebody rendered another uncached image — and Preferences, the one page where the switch is thrown, shows no source artwork at all, so a draining queue would never learn. It is now republished from set_online_sources_enabled beside the existing SET-4 recomputations and after any successful module toggle.
Incident T6-two-agents-one-worktree: running two subagents concurrently in the same worktree, each instructed to run the full workspace gate, was a coordination error. Their uncommitted work compiled together, so neither could get a meaningful result; one deadlocked waiting on its own backgrounded run. Worse, the two failure sets masked each other — a clippy error introduced in 077b32e0 (needless_pass_by_value) went unnoticed because that run died on the other agent's unrelated build errors, and HEAD briefly failed clippy. Either give each agent its own worktree, or let only one run the workspace gate and have the coordinator run the authoritative battery once both have committed.
Note T6-review-open: three reviewer findings are real but are feature work rather than defects, and are NOT fixed. (1) MTP-21 is [aktiv] and requires "jede ungespielte, bereits heruntergeladene Episode" plus counting a wanted-but-undownloaded episode as waiting; the pure projection honours both and is tested, but the live pipeline calls neither — query_candidates_for_device applies no played filter and files_waiting_for_download is hardcoded 0. The rule-named test asserts only the label, so it stays green even if the selection behaviour were removed entirely. With MTP-30 now live this also means a device can read "Up to date" and skip its automatic sync while work is pending. (2) The device view says "Rules from Preferences" but 7b's Phone sync block does not exist, so the referenced controls cannot be reached; SET-8 requires it once Block E exists, and Block E now does. (3) MCP parity does not cover targets, caps, per-category diffs or wanted_on_device — device_dto still exposes only the legacy aggregate, so an agent can start a sync but cannot see what it would do.
Task T6-HDEFG (Block H, MCP parity for D/E/F/G — closes T6-review-open finding 3): complete (this commit, base 87f9e73a, four new MCP tools plus extensions to two existing surfaces, every one reusing the same reprise-core facade the GUI reads/writes rather than a second path). H-D: moved the YouTube channel detail's window/Shorts-filter/download-summary math out of reprise-gnome into podcasts::channel_window (POD-10/POD-11, now [core][gtk]), added the read-only music_get_channel_detail tool on top of it, extracted podcasts::pipeline::download_episode from refresh's inline auto-download loop so a new music_manage_episodes tool's download/remove/want_on_device batch actions and the refresh pipeline share one download path instead of two. H-E: agent_device_sync::AgentDeviceSyncDevice gained a categories field reusing device_sync::SyncTargetKind/CategoryReading directly (no parallel DTO), threaded through a new CategorySnapshot D-Bus method (kept separate from the already-16-wide Snapshot tuple deliberately) into music_get_device_sync_state, which now reports each named target's folder/cap/diff reading plus an aggregate balance computed via the real aggregate_balance function; wanted_on_device (MTP-20) is exposed as music_manage_episodes' want_on_device action, DB-only, no live device needed. H-F: source_data's PodcastEpisodeDto gained download_state and local_availability (NET-3a's real local_availability() bridge, filesystem-checked) so an agent can tell before acting whether an item is already local — documented honestly as the one connectivity signal Reprise can back today, since NET-3c's real OS network-monitor binding does not exist yet. H-G: new music_manage_online_sources tool reads/writes the exact NET-1a/SET-8 authorities (online_sources::is_enabled/set_enabled, modules::is_enabled/set_enabled). No new ux-rules.md rule was needed — MTP-18/20/22, NET-1a, NET-3a and POD-10/11 were already surface-neutral; only POD-11 gained a [core] tag plus a documentation note now that its projection also lives in core. Found and fixed one response-hygiene gap before it shipped: a failed download's raw provider-error message (which can echo the request URL, and an episode audio_url can carry a private token exactly like a feed URL can, SRC-5) was going straight into the tool response; now sanitized to a fixed string, with a dedicated leak test. format, strict clippy (both without and with --features reprise-mcp/mpris), the full unfiltered workspace suite in both configurations (reprise-core 1607, reprise-gnome 1115 non-display + 328 display-only ignores, reprise-mcp 34 unit + full integration suite, reprise-platform-linux 106, reprise-stems 49, reprise-cli 11+2+4+5+8+7+4), core purity, UX traceability at 271 active rules, architecture, accessibility, input parity, and audit with only accepted RUSTSEC-2024-0436 all passed.
Task T6-E8 (7a/7e, sync rules onto the device page): complete (commit daa8246e, merged as 5b38459d, base 8e86a485, follows owner decisions E-5 and E-6 of 2026-07-29 — exactly one MTP device, so the global-versus-per-device split loses the justification it was built on and the rules move to where the device is; the cap became an editable SpinButton wired to SyncTarget::cap_bytes with a re-plan, the selection summary became a live per-device count from the new podcasts::phone_sync::selection_summary instead of the static "Rules from Preferences" stub, and the "Same on all devices" cross-reference is gone along with the never-built Phone sync block; MTP-28 is [ersetzt durch MTP-37] and SET-8 by SET-9, with every rule-named test re-pointed in the same commit and no orphan mtp_28_/set_8_ names left, verified independently; the cap test proves behaviour rather than existence — lowering the cap evicts the older episode from the next plan, red at "left: 2 right: 1" without the fix; the data model was deliberately NOT refactored, because storing per device stays right even when managing several devices does not)
Note T6-zero-meaning: two adjacent numeric settings now give 0 opposite meanings — a cap of 0 GiB means unlimited (cap_bytes has been Option since MTP-18), while MTP-36's decision made a channel's "latest N" of 0 mean "nothing from this channel", on the argument that unlimited must not be selectable or one number can quietly fill the device. That argument applies to the cap just as well, so MTP-36 is the outlier rather than E8's work. MTP-36 is still [geplant] and unimplemented, so aligning it costs one line of rule text. Raised with the owner, not decided locally.
Incident T6-tmpfs: /tmp (a 16G tmpfs, i.e. RAM) filled to 100% mid-session and failed unrelated commands with ENOSPC — 8450 stale tmp.* directories holding 15G. First diagnosis was wrong: AGENTS.md's isolation recipe looked like the culprit, but check-display-tests.sh does clean up its worker roots. The real defect is narrower: the worker drops the parent's EXIT trap so it never deletes a sibling's log, and was then left with no cleanup at all, so the tidy-up ran only on the normal path. Every interrupted or timed-out gate run therefore abandoned five directories per test, about 955 per run — and this session aborted several. Fixed in d6a5b299 by giving each worker a trap for its own five directories on INT/TERM/HUP as well as EXIT, since bash runs no EXIT trap for an untrapped fatal signal and the aborted run is precisely the case that leaked. The improvement could not be measured cleanly while another agent ran the unfixed script concurrently; it is correct by construction and will show on the next aborted run.
Lesson T6-selfkill: `pgrep -f check-display-tests` matches the agent's own shell, whose command line contains that string. It killed this session's own command once (exit 144) and later reported a gate as running when none was. Match on `comm` plus an explicit self-PID exclusion, or accept both a self-kill and a false positive.
Note T6-mtp-renumber: our MTP-18, MTP-19, MTP-20 and MTP-21 are now MTP-38, MTP-39, MTP-40 and MTP-41. Both this branch and origin/dev appended four rules to a common base of MTP-17 without knowing about each other, so the same four IDs carry two different meanings: dev 's are step naming, failure fallout, the sync run log and proven transfer, ours are the three named sync targets, cap eviction, wanted_on_device and the intended-file-set projection. dev is the pushed trunk and its IDs already appear in merged test names, so this branch moves. Our MTP-22 through MTP-37 keep their numbers because dev never used them, which leaves the block non-contiguous — MTP-17, then dev's 18-21, then ours 22-41 — and that is the honest shape rather than a tidy one. Every rule-named test and every code and plan cross-reference moved in this same commit; the entries above this line deliberately were NOT rewritten, because the ledger records what was true when it was written and a renumbered history would misrepresent it. Read any MTP-18 through MTP-21 above this line as MTP-38 through MTP-41. The renumber had to precede the dev merge: dev's own sync-log files reference MTP-20 meaning dev's rule, so a rewrite afterwards could not have told the two apart.
Merge T6-dev: origin/dev merged into feature/podcast-channel-redesign (this merge commit, 53 commits incoming, 18 conflicting files). Two structural collisions dominated it and neither was a text conflict. First, dev's 7d8a47c1 had translated docs/ux-rules.md into English including its status vocabulary ([aktiv]->[active], [geplant]->[planned], [ersetzt durch X]->[replaced by X]) and moved both check-ux-traceability.sh and check-display-tests.sh onto the English tokens; our 40 new rules were German, so a naive merge would have left them unparsed and the traceability gate would have reported ok while looking at none of them, which is the exact failure dev's own commit message warns about. The rulebook was therefore rebuilt from dev's English version and our 40 additions plus 12 edits to pre-existing rules were re-applied translated, giving 405 unique ids with no duplicates and no German marker left. Second, both lines had appended MTP-18 through MTP-21 to a common base of MTP-17 for different rules; ours were renumbered to MTP-38..41 in the separate preceding commit bd4c1ba3, deliberately before the merge, because dev's sync-log files reference MTP-20 meaning dev's rule and a rewrite afterwards could not have told the two apart. Schema: both sides added a v40 migration, so dev's sync log moved to v45 and SUPPORTED_SCHEMA_VERSION is 45. Engine: dev refactored device_sync_planned.rs onto a DeviceSyncMachine reducer, so our per-target path and storage routing was re-applied on top of it and the additive podcast/YouTube content targets now run as a phase after the machine reports Finished rather than interleaved, because the mirror is authoritative over its folder (MTP-17) and the content targets are not - letting the authoritative step settle first means a content copy cannot be deleted by a mirror cleanup in the same run; our device_sync_music_transfer.rs was deleted since the machine owns that work now. MCP: dev replaced the positional D-Bus tuples with named dict types, which removes the zvariant tuple-arity ceiling that was the stated reason our Block H CategorySnapshot was a separate D-Bus method, so categories moved into DeviceSnapshot as a field and the extra method is gone; PROTOCOL_VERSION is 1.4. Four files were split to stay under the 800-line gate (device_sync_browser_tests.rs, device_sync_inflight_tests.rs, device_sync_run_log.rs, and run_content_phase into device_sync_content_transfer.rs). fmt, strict clippy, the full workspace suite with none failing, core purity, UX traceability at 264 active rules, architecture, accessibility semantics, input parity, motion, and audit with only accepted RUSTSEC-2024-0436 all pass. NOT pushed - the owner tests manually first.
Decision T6-zero: the owner decided on 2026-07-29 that 0 means unlimited in every numeric sync setting. The two adjacent numbers on the device page read 0 in opposite directions - the size cap has meant unlimited since MTP-38 modelled cap_bytes as an Option, while MTP-36's "latest N" was drafted with 0 meaning nothing from this channel. MTP-36 was the outlier and still unimplemented, so alignment cost one line of rule text instead of a rebuild. "Nothing from this channel" is what the 6b channel toggle says; it is not a quantity, so it is not expressed as one. Recorded in MTP-36 and in the plan's section 8a as E-9.
Lesson T6-schema-guard: the runtime protocol's no_snapshot_carries_a_local_filesystem_path test bans any snapshot string starting with "/", and the new target_path field legitimately carries a device path like /Podcasts/Reprise, so the field had to be exempted. The first exemption was written with a positive counter-check that looked right and passed - and would have passed forever, because zvariant serializes each field as {"signature","value"} and the checker only collected plain strings, so it inspected nothing. It was caught by deliberately injecting /home/marvin/Music into target_path and observing the test stay green. The rule this branch already wrote for MTP-21 applies to guards as much as to features: after writing one, break the thing it guards and watch it go red.
Fix T6-split-cohesion: the dev merge pushed device_sync_planned.rs over the 800-line file-size gate, and the script that split out the MTP-20 run log took the whole `impl DeviceSyncRuntime` block with it rather than just reload_sync_history — because it looked for the next line that is exactly "}" at column 0, which is the end of the impl, not the end of the method. sync_now, the sync entry point, therefore came to live in a file whose module documentation says it is about recording runs. It compiled, clippy was clean and every test passed, so no gate disagreed; only reading the file for an unrelated reason surfaced it. sync_now and eject are back in device_sync_planned.rs (792 lines) and device_sync_run_log.rs keeps only the log plus its reader (127 lines). The lesson is narrow and mechanical: a brace-matching split must anchor on the item it is moving, not on the first dedented brace after it.
Incident T6-gate-contention: the post-merge display gate reported "failed: 4 of 196" — three stats_17 entrance tests and stats_10_narrow_window. All four pass in isolation under the gate's own environment recipe. Neither this branch nor its side of the merge ever touched crates/reprise-gnome/src/ui/stats/; dev touched it, and did so specifically to fix display-test flakes (ce633e08, af597def). A second session's display gate was running against a different worktree on the same machine throughout the run, which is the CPU-contention condition the fil_9 correction and the cpu-leak incident already identified for this class of timing- and layout-sensitive test. Treated as contention rather than regression only after the isolation runs, and to be confirmed by a repeat gate on a quiet machine rather than by the reasoning alone.
Lesson T6-selfwatch: a background watcher written as `until ! pgrep -f "check-display-tests.sh --rule-named"; do sleep 30; done` never terminates, because the watcher's own command line contains that string and pgrep matches it. This is the same self-match trap recorded as T6-selfkill, walked into again from the other direction: the first time it killed a shell, this time it produced a watcher that would have waited forever while the gate it watched had already finished. Wait on a specific pid with `kill -0` instead, or exclude the caller's own pid explicitly.
Lesson T6-gate-tree: editing tracked sources while a display gate runs invalidates that gate. Each worker re-invokes `cargo test -p reprise-gnome`, so a mid-run edit means some tests ran against the old tree and some against the new one, and cargo's build lock serialises the workers during the rebuild. The rule that two agents must not share a worktree applies to an agent and a running gate just as much.
Confirmation T6-gate-contention: the repeat display gate on a quiet machine reports "passed: 196, failed: 0 of 196". The four stats failures of the earlier run were CPU contention from a second session's gate running concurrently against another worktree, not a merge regression — now established by a clean repeat rather than by the reasoning alone. Note the count moved from 191 pre-merge to 196: dev brought five further display tests with it.
Task T6-E9-prep (design 7f's preparation phase, MTP-42): complete (this commit, base c111ff72, new dependency-pure `reprise_core::device_sync::preparation` decides, from `MTP-41`'s waiting set, `NET-1a`'s global gate, `NET-3a`'s connectivity and the device's own prepare-before-sync switch, whether a preparation phase exists at all and what it does — `Absent` when online sources are off (wins over every other condition, tested stacked against missing files, offline and switch-off together), `NothingMissing`, `SkippedOffline` while offline (also tested against metered-and-switch-off stacked on top, since offline must win over policy, not just appear first in the code), `Offered` when metered or the switch is off, otherwise `Planned`; `primary_action` projects only `Planned` to "Download & sync" and leaves every other phase, including `Offered`, as a plain "Sync now" — an enum, not a display string, wording stays in the frontend. Added MTP-42 to docs/ux-rules.md [active][core] with its precedence and the offline-beats-metered reasoning as a doc comment. format, strict clippy, the full unfiltered workspace suite (reprise-core 1690 passed incl. 11 new mtp_42_ tests, reprise-gnome 1109 passed + 334 display-only ignores, reprise-platform-linux 117, reprise-stems 49, reprise-cli 11+2+4+5+8+7+4+13+4+2+7+0+0, reprise-mcp 34 unit + full integration suite, reprise-runtime 72 + 4 protocol + 8 schema), core purity (cargo tree -p reprise-core has no gtk4/libadwaita/gstreamer/zbus), UX traceability at 265 active rules, architecture, accessibility semantics, input parity, and audit with only accepted RUSTSEC-2024-0436 all passed. Housekeeping: this worktree's own git branch had been provisioned from a stale, unrelated base (a `dev` merge missing the entire E7/E8/connectivity lineage this task needed); reset in place to feature/podcast-channel-redesign's tip (c111ff72) before starting, losing nothing unique since the sole prior commit was a redundant PR merge already in origin/dev.
Task T6-E9-A/B (7f, preparation projection and download priority): complete (commits d4bf6c04 and 60fb26f4, merged as 2db8ce31, base c111ff72, built by two subagents in separate worktrees and verified here by mutation rather than by their reports). MTP-42 is the pure projection reprise_core::device_sync::preparation: five states (Absent, NothingMissing, SkippedOffline, Offered, Planned) resolved in a fixed order where NET-1a's global gate beats everything, then nothing-missing, then offline, then metered-or-switch-off, then planned. Offline deliberately beats metered because offline is a fact about whether the download can run while metered is policy about whether it should, and offering a download that cannot run either way would be a lie dressed as a choice. MTP-44 gives the existing download manager a priority lane rather than a second path: PodcastsRequest carries a priority, the one worker drains the priority channel first via futures_lite::future::or, and PodcastsOperation::Download and pipeline remain the single operation and single executor; a DownloadForSync variant was considered and rejected as a second path in spirit. Verified by mutation: removing the NET-1a gate turned two mtp_42_ tests red, removing the offline branch turned four red, and reverting the priority lane to FIFO turned two mtp_44_ tests red. fmt, strict clippy, the full workspace suite with none failing, core purity, UX traceability at 266 active rules, architecture, accessibility semantics, input parity, motion and audit with only accepted RUSTSEC-2024-0436 all pass.
Fix T6-E9-hang: the first version of the MTP-44 tests called the blocking recv_prefer_priority directly. That proved the behaviour but failed in the wrong way — reverting the priority lane to FIFO drains the ordinary lane and then waits forever on an empty one, so the mutation run sat for thirteen minutes instead of reporting anything. A gate that hangs reports nothing, which for a rule whose whole purpose is to keep a second download path from drifting in unnoticed is the one failure mode that must not happen. The receive now runs behind a five-second deadline on a detached thread; detached rather than std::thread::scope, because scope joins before returning and would hang on exactly the stuck receive the deadline exists to catch. Confirmed in both directions: unmutated 3 passed in 0.00s, mutated 2 failed in 5.00s naming the cause (commit 17ae47a1). The general lesson is that "would this test go red if I deleted the feature" is not sufficient on its own — it also has to go red rather than silent.
Note T6-two-download-paths: E9's binding constraint is that preparation reuses the existing download manager with no second path. It is satisfied to the letter — no new path was added — but not in spirit, because a second path already existed before E9. reprise_core::podcasts::pipeline::download_episode is used by the refresh auto-download loop and by MCP's music_manage_episodes, while reprise-gnome's own try_download_episode_to_root serves the GUI's manual download with its own episode lookup, its own NET-1a source-kind gate and its own subscription lookup. Block H extracted the shared path for refresh and MCP but left the GTK worker on its copy. E9's preparation now sits on the GTK copy. Converging the two is a refactor with real risk (the GUI path's per-source-kind gating and progress emission must survive) and is deliberately NOT folded into E9; raised with the owner as its own decision rather than decided locally.
Lesson T6-selfkill-again: the pgrep self-match trap recorded as T6-selfkill was hit twice more in this session, from both directions — once as a watcher written `until ! pgrep -f "check-display-tests.sh --rule-named"` that could never terminate because its own command line matched, and once as `pkill -f "verify_e9.sh"` that killed the calling shell with exit 144. Reading the earlier entry was not enough to prevent either. The durable form of the rule: never pass a pattern to pgrep/pkill that appears in the invoking command line — wait on a captured pid with `kill -0`, and kill by pid. Also note that a pid captured by `pgrep | head -1` right after `setsid nohup` can be a short-lived child rather than the script, which made one watcher fire immediately against a gate that was still running.
Lesson T6-codex-stdin: a `codex exec` invocation appeared to run for one hour fifty-five minutes at zero percent CPU. It was not reviewing anything; it sat on "Reading additional input from stdin..." because stdin was left open. Non-interactive tool runs need `< /dev/null` (or an explicit piped prompt). Elapsed time alone is not evidence of work — check CPU and the output stream before assuming progress.
Review T6-codex-merge: an external reviewer (Codex CLI, read-only, no builds) audited 3559f0c8..70890178 — the dev merge plus the rule renumber — and returned eight findings. Six were real and are fixed in b441b400; two were already addressed by later commits the review could not see because it was pinned to the merge commit. Every finding was verified against the code before being acted on, and the two most serious were mine, introduced when the content phase was re-applied on top of dev's DeviceSyncMachine. (1) P1, the compile break: DeviceSnapshot gained a mandatory categories field but two reprise-mcp struct literals omit it, which `cargo check --workspace --all-targets` cannot see because the mpris feature is off by default — AGENTS.md's Block H entry calls for building both configurations and that had been dropped; the mpris build is now part of the battery. (2) P1, data loss: a failed content copy no longer stopped the phases after it, so a failure to copy a new episode could still be followed by a cap-eviction removal (MTP-39/MTP-25) deleting the older resident episode it was meant to replace — the device loses a file and gains nothing. The pre-machine implementation gated every step on failures.is_empty() and that guard was lost in the rewrite. (3) P1, cancellation swallowed: cancelling mid-phase only broke the inner loops and then returned the mirror's original Completed outcome, so finish_sync closed the run log as a success and cleared reconnect resumability. (4) P2, progress restarted at zero although the plan total already includes content bytes, so a mixed run could reach 100% and jump back. (5) P2, every Ok(_) from a removal counted as a deletion although the backend returns Ok(false) for an already-missing file, and content failures never reached the run log at all. (6) P3, a dangling NET-3c cross-reference and two helpers left public beyond their file. All three P1s had passed the entire gate battery: fmt, clippy, the full workspace suite, five script gates and a 196-of-196 display gate. This is the second external review on this branch to find defects the gates let through, and the pattern is worth stating plainly: gates catch what they were written to catch.
Task T6-E9-regression (MTP-23): complete (commit 0f02836d, merged as 1b509ff0, built by a subagent in its own worktree). Two regression tests for the P1 content-phase defects: a failed content copy must stop the later removal phases, and cancelling mid-phase must not be recorded as a completed run. The FakeBackend gained with_replace_track_error in the same style as the existing with_*_error builders so a content copy can be made to fail on demand, and the cancellation test uses a one-second bounded poll rather than an unbounded await, per the lesson in 17ae47a1. Verified independently after the merge by mutating this branch's own implementation: removing the may_continue guards fails exactly the removal test, removing the Cancelled return fails exactly the cancellation test, neither mutation disturbs the other. fmt, strict clippy, the full workspace suite, core purity, 266 active rules, four script gates, the mpris configuration and audit all pass.
Note T6-agent-briefing: the regression-test agent was told its two target defects were already fixed on its base branch. They were not — the fixes were sitting uncommitted in the coordinating session's working tree, so the agent's base legitimately still contained both bugs. It checked the claim against git history rather than trusting the brief, reported the contradiction, and implemented the fixes itself to keep the suite green. That is the right behaviour and cost only duplicated work, which was reconciled by keeping the coordinator's superset and taking the agent's tests. The lesson is for the coordinator: commit before briefing an agent about the state of the branch, because "it is on the branch" is a claim about git, not about a working tree.
Task E9 (design 7f's visible two-phase sync surface, MTP-43): complete (this commit, base 17ae47a1, builds on MTP-42's `preparation::plan_preparation`/`primary_action` and MTP-44's priority lane without re-deriving either decision). Core: `DeviceSettings` gained `prepare_before_sync` (migration v46, `SUPPORTED_SCHEMA_VERSION` bumped to 46, backfilled to `true` for existing rows) — the stored value is only ever what the user chose; offline/metered overrides stay `plan_preparation`'s job, never a mutation of this field. GTK: new `device_sync_preparation.rs` gathers facts in `recompute_delta_silent` (the missing-episode list, real `gio::NetworkMonitor` connectivity/metered, `online_sources::is_enabled`) and orchestrates the download-then-sync run through a new `PreparationDownloader` seam — production adapts `PodcastsRuntime` onto MTP-44's `High` priority lane (the one `PodcastsOperation::Download`, no second path), tests substitute a recording fake so the switch's actual dispatch behaviour is provable without a real worker thread or a channel a regression could hang on. `sync_now` now branches on `primary_action` before either starting a preparation run (`begin_prepared_sync`) or going straight to the renamed `start_transfer_now`; cancelling flips a flag the download loop checks between files, so an already-requested download is never interrupted or rolled back and `wanted_on_device` is never cleared anywhere in this path (verified by grep, not just by argument). The preparation overview (episode titles + "N files to download · SIZE", hidden outright for `Absent`/`NothingMissing`), the switch beside `remove_deleted`/`sync_automatically`, the primary button's "Download & sync" label, the "Step 1 of 2"/"Step 2 of 2" two-phase progress reading, and the `SkippedOffline` "N episodes skipped" note are wired into the page/layout/content-panel/strings modules. Known gap, flagged rather than papered over: no feed or provider in this codebase persists an expected byte size for an undownloaded episode (RSS enclosure `length` and YouTube size are both parsed nowhere), so `MissingFile::size_bytes` is honestly `0` and the overview omits the byte figure instead of showing "0 B" — the file count is still real. Four files were split to stay under the 800-line gate (`device_sync_page_copy.rs` and `device_sync_page_display_tests.rs` out of the page/its tests, `device_sync_effects.rs` out of `device_sync_planned.rs`), plus one line trimmed from `window.rs` to stay under its separate 600-line composition-root cap. Added MTP-43 `[active][gtk]` to docs/ux-rules.md between MTP-42 and MTP-44. format, strict clippy (-D warnings), the full unfiltered workspace suite (reprise-core 1692 passed incl. new `prepare_before_sync`/migration/`preparation_overview` tests, reprise-gnome 1121 passed incl. the two behavioral `mtp_43_` tests in `device_sync_preparation_tests.rs` + 334 display-only ignores, every other crate green), core purity, UX traceability at 267 active rules, architecture, accessibility semantics, input parity, and audit with only accepted RUSTSEC-2024-0436 all passed. Housekeeping: this worktree's own branch had also been provisioned from a stale, unrelated base; reset in place to feature/podcast-channel-redesign's tip (17ae47a1) before starting.
Task T6-E9-C (7f, the visible two-phase sync surface): complete (commit b0f33d6a, merged as fbafe7d4, built by a subagent in its own worktree, verified here rather than taken on report). MTP-43 gives the device page the preparation overview with episode titles, the "Download missing files before syncing" switch (DeviceSettings::prepare_before_sync, schema v46, stored default true), the primary button reading "Download & sync" exactly when MTP-42's projection says so, the two-phase "Step 1 of 2 · Downloading N of M · P%" reading, and the SkippedOffline note. Preparation dispatches through a PreparationDownloader seam that production adapts onto MTP-44's High priority lane, so there is still one download operation and one executor. Cancelling flips a flag checked between files and never touches an in-flight or completed download, and never clears wanted_on_device. Verified by mutation: making plan_preparation ignore prepare_switch_on fails exactly mtp_43_the_switch_actually_gates_whether_a_preparation_download_starts and nothing else — the switch is read, not merely stored, which is the third time this branch has had to prove that explicitly. fmt, strict clippy, the full workspace suite, core purity, 267 active rules, five script gates, the mpris configuration and audit all pass. E9 is now complete across MTP-42, MTP-43 and MTP-44.
Note T6-E9-C-open: two limitations the agent flagged rather than papered over, both real and both deliberately left. (1) Nothing in this codebase knows an episode's size before it is downloaded — no RSS enclosure length is parsed and no YouTube size is fetched — so MissingFile::size_bytes is honestly 0 and the overview reads "N files to download", appending a size only when a real one is known. Design 7f's example line says "2 files to download · 312 MiB"; a fabricated number would have matched the mockup and lied, so the number is omitted instead. Real size estimation needs RSS enclosure parsing plus a schema column and is its own task. (2) Connectivity and metered are read directly from gio::NetworkMonitor in device_sync_compact, following the existing podcast_refresh_scheduler pattern. That contradicts NET-3a, which states connectivity is an explicitly set value and never an inference, and which RadioView already honours through set_connectivity. The behavioural test carries a sanity assertion so a machine without network fails loudly instead of passing for the wrong reason, but the inconsistency stands and should be closed by making connectivity injectable here too.
Incident T6-parallel-field: merging E9-C exposed a break neither subagent could have seen. E9-C added a field to DeviceSettings; the regression-test agent, working in parallel from an earlier base, constructs that struct. Each was green on its own base and the pair does not compile. Worse, the first verification run after the merge printed no test result at all for the step that was supposed to prove the switch behaves — compilation had already failed — and a reader skimming for a FAILED line would have called it green. That is the same shape as the earlier grep-for-FAILED mistake: the absence of an error message is not evidence of success. The coordinator running the full battery after combining parallel work is what caught it, and the check for "did the step actually produce output" belongs in that habit.
Task T6-D4 (6b, download errors classified and sanitized, closes #106): complete (this commit, base 89e3e7e6). Added `PodcastError::classify` in `reprise-core::podcasts` — the single fixed-string classifier (timed out / could not be reached / HTTP error / invalid data / not modified / yt-dlp / disabled) that already existed as MCP's private `source_actions::podcast_source_error` match; that function now delegates to it instead of keeping a second copy. `pipeline::download_episode`'s failure branch and its `tracing::warn!` log line both switched from `error.to_string()`/`%error` to the classified reason, closing the actual leak: a `PodcastError::Transport`/`Body`/`YtDlp` payload can echo the raw provider transport error (which can carry the request URL and, per SRC-5, a private per-subscriber token) or, for yt-dlp's `finalize_download`, a local download path via `Path::display()`. Found and fixed a second, undocumented copy of the whole download executor: `reprise-gnome`'s `podcasts_worker.rs::try_download_episode_to_root` — the code the "Download episode" button and MTP-44's high-priority preparation lane actually call — reimplements `download_episode` rather than calling it, so it had the identical unsanitized `error.to_string()` leak independently; classified it with the same `PodcastError::classify()` rather than merging the two executors (out of scope here, flagged instead — see below). One pre-existing test (`pod_7_failed_worker_download_emits_failed_and_removes_partial`) was asserting the *raw* leaked string as correct behaviour and had to be corrected to the classified one. GTK: `podcasts_groups.rs`'s Failed row used to hide its reason behind `set_tooltip_text` only — a pointer-only affordance this repo's accessibility/input-parity policy forbids — now the classified reason is a second, always-visible label next to the fixed "Download failed" heading; the retry action gets a distinct icon/tooltip (`view-refresh-symbolic` / "Retry download") instead of looking like a first-time download. Retry and fresh-provider-output were already correct (a Failed episode never has `downloaded_path` set, so `toggle_download` re-enters the plain download branch and reruns `queued`→`downloading`→`downloaded` with a real fetcher call, no caching), as was `.part`/postprocessor-leftover cleanup (`download_atomically` and `ytdlp_download::cleanup_artifacts` already swept them on failure) — verified with a new failure-path test for the yt-dlp leftover case, which had no prior coverage. Added `POD-13` `[active][core][gtk]` to `docs/ux-rules.md` after `POD-12`. Split `ytdlp.rs`'s inline test module into `ytdlp_tests.rs` (its own new test pushed the file to 819 lines) to stay under the 800-line architecture gate. New `pod_13_*` tests include a redaction test that feeds a signed URL with a query string, a credential and an absolute local path through both the GUI worker and the shared pipeline and asserts none of it survives into `DownloadState::Failed` or a captured `tracing` log line (checked by temporarily reverting the fix — confirmed it fails with the leaking substrings, then restored). format, strict clippy (`-D warnings`), the full unfiltered workspace suite (reprise-core 1696, reprise-gnome 1135 + 336 display-only ignores, reprise-mcp 121, every other crate green — all 0 failed), core purity, UX traceability at 268 active rules, architecture, accessibility semantics, input parity, the mpris MCP check and audit (only accepted RUSTSEC-2024-0436) all passed.
Note T6-D4-open: `podcasts_worker.rs`'s manual-download executor duplicating `pipeline::download_episode` (not merely sharing its message classifier) predates this task and was not unified here — doing so safely would mean reconciling several existing rule-named tests (`net_1a_download_is_blocked_when_its_source_kind_is_disabled`, `pod_7_download_worker_emits_ordered_monotone_states_and_persists_after_publish`, the `MTP-44` priority tests) against `pipeline::download_episode`'s slightly different contract (it does not itself gate `NET-1a`, its `EpisodeNotFound` path emits no progress states at all). Flagged rather than silently worked around; a genuine merge is a separate, riskier task. Both copies now share the same classifier, so they cannot drift on redaction even though they remain two executors.
Task T6-followups (B2, B3, D2, D3, D4, G2, MTP-36, download convergence): complete (merged into dev as separate squashes 4565720f, e45e997f, df2d8d1f, afe27632, 138992f8, a3bcc7a0, 699ff39e; built by seven subagents in their own worktrees and verified here by mutation rather than by their reports). Each is described in its own pull request; what belongs in the ledger is what the work uncovered rather than what it added. Three of the seven found a live defect nobody had gone looking for. MTP-36: the selection did `take(latest)`, so a channel configured with 0 silently stopped syncing instead of syncing without a limit — the owner's zero decision that morning had been treated as one line of rule text and turned out to describe a real bug. POD-13/D4: a failed download's raw provider message reached both `DownloadState::Failed` and the warn log, and a podcast `audio_url` can carry a private per-subscriber token (SRC-5); the visible reason was also tooltip-only, so it was unreachable by keyboard and screen reader. B2: the podcasts filter row lived inside the swappable stack page, so switching to the no-results state hid the one control a user needs to undo the filter that emptied the list. None of the three was part of its task; each surfaced because the brief required proving the behaviour rather than asserting it — the zero case, the leak case, the visibility difference.
Note T6-convergence-gate: converging the two download executors onto `pipeline::download_episode` revealed that the core function had no NET-1a gate at all. Its two existing callers were each gated before calling in — refresh only reaches it after a same-kind fetch already passed, and MCP checks itself — so the privacy gate was a property of the callers, not of the download. A naive convergence would have deleted the GNOME copy that held the check and left the core path ungated, while the existing `net_1a_` test went green because it covered the deleted code. The gate now lives inside `download_episode` at the same position in the state sequence, so a refusal still emits Queued → Downloading{0,None} → Failed. Proven by forcing the gate open and watching the rule-named test run into an `unreachable!()` double.
Incident T6-my-battery: a private gate battery was used all day instead of `scripts/check-merge-readiness.sh`, the script CI itself runs through `ci-quality.sh`. It had five of that script's checks. `check-frontend-thinness.sh` was among the missing ones, the turn 6/7 merge pushed reprise-gnome's rusqlite budget from 515 to 563, and dev failed that gate from the merge until the owner repaired it in 323c5828. Every "all gates green" claim made before that repair was unsupported for four checks. The lesson is not "remember the gate" but "do not rebuild a gate the repository already defines" — the approximation is what made the omission invisible. Note also that `check-merge-readiness.sh` currently refuses to start for every branch, because `main` is not an ancestor of `dev`; the one-time `-s ours` repair is documented in docs/agents/branching.md and reserved for the owner, so its checks are being run individually until then.
Lesson T6-silent-verification: four times in one session a verification step produced no output and was nearly read as success — a mutation test whose build failed to compile, a `--workspace` test filter that matched nothing, a `tail` that truncated the failing step out of a battery's output, and a gate summary that said HAS FAILURES while ten visible lines said passed. Each time the step had not run at all. The rule that survives: a check that printed nothing did not happen, and the absence of an error message is not evidence. Read the numbers, not the silence — this is the same shape as the earlier grep-for-FAILED mistake and it recurs under time pressure.
Lesson T6-agent-seams: seven subagents working in parallel from a base that moved underneath them produced no defect inside any one contribution, and four at the seams between them: two agents claimed POD-13 for different rules, two independently created the same test file to satisfy the file-size gate, one sanitized a download executor another had already deleted, and one added a struct field another's fixture then failed to construct. All four were invisible to the agents (each was green on its own base) and to their gates, and were caught only by the coordinator running the full battery after combining the work. Parallel agents need disjoint rule ids assigned up front, and the integrator must treat every combination as unverified until it is verified as a whole.
Lesson T6-worktree-base: every agent worktree on this repository is provisioned from `main`, not from the current branch, so each of the seven began with `git reset --hard origin/dev`. That is not incidental — `main` carries a promotion merge commit dev has never seen, which is the same topology fact that stops `check-merge-readiness.sh` from starting. Until the trunk repair lands, every agent brief must say so in its first paragraph rather than as an aside.
Review T6-codex-followups: a second external review (Codex CLI, read-only) audited 3559f0c8..dev after the seven follow-up blocks landed, and was pointed at the seams between parallel contributions rather than at the contributions themselves — because in the first review two of three P1s had come from a conflict resolution, not from an author. It returned two P1 and six P2 findings, every one real, none of which any gate had caught. P1: "keep last N downloaded" ranked all episodes of a subscription and applied the downloaded filter afterwards, so undownloaded episodes consumed rank positions — a show with twenty episodes where the user had downloaded the three oldest gave those ranks 18 to 20 and keep-5 deleted all three, silently, with no error and the files simply gone. It predates the per-channel work, which made it worse by letting the user choose an N that still meant something else. P1: MTP-41 claimed YouTube limits each channel to its latest N "regardless of download state" while the code deliberately admitted only downloaded or explicitly wanted episodes; both positions were defensible so it was raised rather than decided locally, and the owner ruled the code right and the rule wrong (recorded as E-10). P2: a local path in a normal-level log line, raw yt-dlp stderr rendered in the add dialog's preview, and "unlimited" displayed to the user as "latest 0 each" — the device page reporting nothing selected while everything was. P2: two rule-named tests that would not have noticed their own feature being deleted. All fixed; the destructive one verified twice, once by its author and once here by putting the bug back.
Lesson T6-unwatched-tests: the two weak rule-named tests the second review found were both written the same day, under briefs that explicitly demanded a test go red if its behaviour were removed. POD-13's retry test asserted the button's icon and tooltip and never activated it; POD-9's tests covered the computation but never that it reached the surface. The requirement was stated, accepted, and not met, and nobody noticed because nobody asked for the evidence. The durable form of the rule is therefore not "require the test to fail without its feature" but "require the red output to be shown" — an unwatched test is indistinguishable from a test that does nothing, and stating the requirement more firmly does not close that gap. Every rule-named test on this branch is now expected to arrive with its actual failure message from the sabotaged run.
Note T6-final-state: dev carries the complete turn 6/7 plan (blocks A through H, E1 through E9), every follow-up (B2, B3, D2, D3, D4, F2, F4, G2, MTP-36), the download-executor convergence, and both review rounds. Issue #106's fix is on dev (POD-13) but the issue is still open: the `closes #106` keyword only fires when a branch merges into the default branch, which here is main, so nothing on dev can close it and it must be closed by hand or by the trunk repair. Two gaps are deliberate and recorded rather than hidden: neither keep_downloaded nor latest_per_channel has a UI, so both are settable only through the store, and NET-3 point 2's dimmed "Needs network" rows are unwired, with the rule text saying so. The location hoist is likewise half done — storage is app-level, but its settings rows still live on the Concerts preferences page, so a radio user is deep-linked into Concerts to set it. Verification throughout was local only: GitHub Actions could not run for any of it (an account billing condition), and scripts/check-merge-readiness.sh refuses to start for every branch until the one-time `-s ours` trunk repair documented in docs/agents/branching.md, so its fifteen checks were run individually before each merge. The rule-named display gate ends at 214 of 214. No manual acceptance has happened yet; MTP-30 in particular means a connected phone with pending work begins transferring unprompted.

Task R8: complete (commits 4c29059ffa, 3808628077 and 18b35362ef, base 2319b19f53, added bounded revision-stamped pages for both queue sections and an absolute seek command across protocol 4.2, runtime, client and Linux service; whole-queue change detection now invalidates positions beyond the 200-row snapshot; the adversarial review fixed zero-duration episodes being collapsed to the start, pinned the distinct SeekTo wire method and target, corrected the protocol wording and repaired two inherited dev gate drifts; the two unknown-duration regressions and the wire contract were each mutation-proven red then green; format, strict workspace clippy, warning-free workspace rustdoc, the complete workspace suite with only the two sandbox-blocked MCP Radio listener tests excluded, architecture, runtime-service packaging, frontend thinness, UX traceability, motion, accessibility, input parity, QA linters, file-size enforcement and cached audit with only accepted RUSTSEC-2024-0436 passed)
Assumption R8: a non-live item with `duration_ms == 0` has an unknown upper bound, so the runtime preserves the non-negative seek target and lets the backend, which knows the actual stream length, accept or refuse it; live streams remain structurally non-seekable.
Manual R8: the real private-D-Bus queue-page and absolute-seek integration tests could not start because this sandbox rejects Unix-socket binding with `Operation not permitted`; the full merge-readiness run reached the two inherited MCP Radio fixtures and stopped at their same `TcpListener::bind` restriction before display and runtime-bus phases. R8 changes no GTK rendering or pointer behavior, so no CUA claim is made.

Task POD-migration-v40: complete (commit 8ba7e635, base c18272ef, fixed the defect behind a live report that both the Podcasts and the YouTube page rendered "no such column: sync_to_phone" instead of a library; the owner's database reports user_version 48, a fully migrated schema, yet lacks exactly the two columns migrate_v40 adds and no others, because that migration alone guarded on the version rather than on the version and its own columns — v34, v43 and v47 all guard on both and therefore repair such a database on the next start, while v40 short-circuited forever. The precondition is this repository's own history: schema numbers were reassigned during development, so a build whose v40 meant something else raised the version past 40 without running this ALTER TABLE. The guard now includes both columns and the version is raised with max() rather than set to 40, which would have rolled a v48 database backwards. Verified three ways: the new test drops both columns from a fully migrated database and is red before the change; the full workspace suite and strict clippy pass; and reprise-cli run against a copy of the affected installation restored both columns, left user_version at 48, and made the failing query return its two subscriptions. Beyond legibility this closes the same leak class POD-13 closes for provider errors, since a statement can carry a path or an identifier. Task POD-ytdlp-part: complete (this commit, base 4745717e, fixed the defect behind a live report that no YouTube episode could be downloaded — every attempt ended in "Download failed · YouTube source could not be read with yt-dlp", logged as failure_kind="other", exit_code=1, with the provider text discarded by POD-13 so the cause was invisible. yt-dlp reserves a trailing `.part` for its own partial downloads: it strips that suffix from the output template, writes the media under the shortened name, and its post-processor then fails to find what it just wrote, ending in exit 1 with "Postprocessing: WARNING: unable to obtain file audio codec with ffprobe". downloads::partial_path appends exactly that suffix because download_atomically needs a temporary name, and the value was handed straight to -o. For RSS this is harmless since Reprise performs that download itself; only the yt-dlp executor passes the name to a foreign tool that reserves it. Isolated against the real binary (2026.07.04): the identical command succeeds with `-o episode.opus` and exits 1 with `-o episode.part`, so the suffix is the whole trigger. The executor now asks for `<temporary>.download`, a name yt-dlp honours literally; it already learns the real output through --print after_move:filepath and finalize_download moves that onto the temporary path, so the atomic-publish contract and every `.part` rule are untouched, and the new name still starts with the temporary path's prefix so cleanup_artifacts keeps sweeping it. Both executors have carried this since 89e3e7e6. Test-first with a fake binary that reproduces the contract rather than the symptom — it refuses any -o ending in `.part` — and verified end to end against the real binary, which returns exit 0 and a genuine Ogg/Opus file for the new path. Format, strict clippy, the full workspace suite at 3515 passing, UX traceability at 275 active rules and the rule-named display gate at "failed: 0 of 217" passed.)
Incident POD-ytdlp-orphans: the failures above left residue that the code cannot see. Because yt-dlp stripped `.part`, it wrote each attempt to the destination path itself, which is the one name cleanup_artifacts does not match — it sweeps by the temporary path's prefix. The owner's library holds eight such files, about 293 MB, against a single row with a downloaded_path. They are not usable: `file` reports WebM, so they are the raw pre-conversion downloads wearing an `.opus` name, and adopting them through reclaim_existing would hand the player and the device sync a mislabelled container. They also block their own episodes after the fix, since download_atomically refuses to publish over an existing destination. The fix makes the situation unreachable going forward — yt-dlp can no longer write to the destination — but the existing files are the user's and were left untouched for them to decide. Worth a separate look: a destination the database does not know about leaves the row permanently on "Download failed" with no way out from the UI, which is a dead end rather than an error.

# Tag Editor save no longer throws the library to the top

Task tag-save-viewport-jump: complete (this commit, base b232cee2f5, saving any tag from the Tag Editor threw the track table to the very top of the library and selected whatever row happened to sit there, before a later reload pulled it back — the reported "jumped to the top and then back"; the cause is not the save reload but `TransientFocusGuard::capture`, which remembered the focused widget itself, and opened from the table that widget is a recycled `GtkColumnView` row: after the save's `items_changed(0, old, new)` the same widget is bound to a different row, so `restore()` focused it and GTK scrolled and selected there; `capture` now remembers the enclosing ColumnView/ListView/GridView instead, which is never recycled and keeps its own focus row, and `try_focus` accepts a focus a container handed on to a child so the fallback no longer fires; this fixes every one of the 26 capture call sites, not just the Tag Editor, so the context menu, delete dialog, playlist export and issue views stop jumping too; three new TAG-1 display regressions sample the vertical adjustment once per rendered frame — the existing TAG-1 test only asserts the settled end state and is blind to a transient jump — and the focus-restore one was observed red at 6662 px dropping to 0 px, green after the fix, and red again in a deliberate counter-check with the fix disabled, in both the debug and release profile and with and without a window manager; the earlier hypotheses of the doubled save reload, the sidebar refresh and a plain `column_view.grab_focus()` were each disproved by a green test rather than assumed; format, strict workspace clippy and the complete workspace suite passed with zero failures; the redundant idle reload in `refresh_after_tag_mutation_with_anchor` remains untouched wasted work and is deliberately left to its own change; no real music file, database or live desktop was touched and nothing was pushed)

Task tag-save-viewport-jump-2: complete (this commit, base 4fc648116f, the first attempt above did NOT fix the reported jump and its green test was measuring the wrong thing: sampling the viewport on the frame clock returns no samples at all when nothing damages the widget, so the assertion passed vacuously; sampling on a timer instead shows the table standing at the top of the library for some 500 ms after every tag save, with or without the earlier `stable_focus_target` change and with or without the doubled reload, so neither was the cause; the actual cause is that `items_changed(0, old, new)` resets a `GtkColumnView`'s focus row to the top, and the dialog's focus restore then makes GTK reveal that row — remembering the view rather than the recycled row never addressed it, and a scroll pin around the focus change was measured to be ineffective and removed rather than kept as insurance; `TransientFocusGuard::restore` now focuses the row already visible at the view's top edge via `ListScrollFlags::FOCUS`, which has nothing to reveal and therefore cannot scroll; the save refresh additionally re-queries once instead of twice, since only the deferred reload can be trusted to rebind rows behind a closing dialog, with one test pinning the query count and another proving the written tag still reaches the visible cells; every display regression was observed red before and green after, and the focus fix was re-checked red with it disabled; format, strict workspace clippy and the complete workspace suite passed with zero failures across 40 suites; the genre column is hidden in the default layout, so the tests edit the album instead — a genre edit would have proven nothing about what reached the cells; no real music file, database or live desktop was touched)
